# Rule ledger — documentation restructure (brief 151)

One line per rule found in the source documents at `e7d143d5`, in source order:

    - <ID> · L<start>-<end> · <rule> · → <new location | dropped: kind>

- Every source line belongs to an entry's line range (blank lines and headings fold into a neighbour).
  Where one source line holds several rules, entries share that line; indented letter-suffixed entries
  (e.g. `UPM-017a`) are further rules on their parent's line.
- `→ docs/<FILE>.md § <heading>`: where the rule now lives. `dropped: history` (dated narrative, ticket or evidence
  record), `duplicate of <ID|file:line>` (the kept copy), `superseded by <ID|file:line>` (older form; current form kept),
  `removed with R1/R2/R3` (design references, public PNGs, Stats prototype — user decisions 10 Oct 2026),
  `outdated (Codex/first-release workflow)` (user decision R7a), `no rule`.
- `[unsure: …]`: kept because it could not be shown to be out of force; listed in the implementer report.
- Line numbers refer to the files as they were at `e7d143d5` (`git show e7d143d5:<path>`).

Page-specific UI rules / Public Board family (Board, TeamBoard, Tile)
- UIS-149 · L913-915 · prefers-reduced-motion disables/minimizes transitions and overlay animation; public toasts pause while hovered/focused and dismiss by Escape or labeled button. · → docs/UI.md § 7. Responsive, accessibility, progressive enhancement
- UIS-150 · L916-919 · SignalR/fetch updates are invalidations only; they must not replace authoritative state or interrupt an active submission or submission-result acknowledgement. · → docs/ARCHITECTURE.md § 11. Realtime
- UIS-151 · L920-933 · Drop announcement: thin coral top border is a 10-second compaction countdown draining full -> empty; focus/interaction resets and holds it full; leaving focus restarts a fresh 10 s; runs only outside hover and keyboard focus; internal focus moves don't release; no focus stealing; touch/keyboard can keep it expanded; reduced motion keeps timer semantics; separate from green "Tile completed" status and the server-saved two-minute expansion cooldown; no second global toast/dialog system. · → docs/UI.md § 9. Page-specific UI rules / Public Drops and drop announcement
- UIS-152 · L934-946 · Replace, do not layer: adopting a primitive removes superseded markup/CSS/JS/aliases/wrappers/dividers/nested surfaces/duplicate headings; every visible surface/border/divider/shadow/pseudo-element has one named owner (exceptions recorded); remaining Bootstrap/local classes must be behaviour-only; a new shared selector does not erase a page-local override, which must be removed or recorded. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-153 · L947-954 · Role/model policy lives in AGENTS.md; the major visual workflow adds no rendered reviewer/readiness review to Admin popup behaviour passes or tiny corrections. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-154 · L955-957 · Every UI task states the exact page/result, markup, classes, states, files and non-goals before implementation. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-155 · L958-966 · A visual family may share one composition across sibling pages; a materially distinct unresolved structure needs a user-supplied picture before implementation; never invent a composition; no redundant screenshots for covered siblings. [unsure: reference library removed with R1/R2; rule now applies to new pages only] · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-156 · L967-975 · Detailed implementer self-inspection only for the first implementation of a page family, on user request, or for a material visual uncertainty; surgical follow-ups implement the finding and run focused checks without subjective iteration; the user does visual acceptance. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-157 · L976-986 · User screenshots are the normal post-implementation evidence (browser chrome/slight viewport variance OK); historical pictures are targets only when the user reactivates them; reviewer compares current evidence plus scoped diff; static editorial copy is not frozen unless approvals say so (keep meaning, truth, localization, dynamic facts, action semantics). · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-158 · L987-994 · Major Admin visual passes are reviewed on rendered routes plus scoped diff against shell, tokens, typography, component owners, layout families, responsive, focus and behaviour; reject legacy/Public residue (blue outlines, Bootstrap-primary, page-local themes); screenshots alone don't establish compliance. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-159 · L995-1000 · Must use: approved family, exact shared owners, focus/feedback behaviour, route fallbacks, server-authoritative forms. Must remove: superseded residue, duplicate wrappers/headings, conflicting local variants, unowned borders/dividers/shadows. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-160 · L1001-1006 · Visual review of a structural rewrite uses rendered evidence (silhouette, typefaces/scale, header geometry, artwork, rhythm, density, actions/icons, colour, whitespace, responsive); source/token/selector/test-only verdicts cannot approve visual fidelity. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-161 · L1007-1010 · May remain: a named page-local pattern or behaviour-only compatibility class explicitly permitted and not conflicting with shared appearance. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-162 · L1011-1021 · Major unapproved UI page: before implementation one independent read-only rendered readiness/residue review produces a numbered contract (residue to remove, owner to adopt, states/files/selectors, protected behaviour, non-goals, objective acceptance) covering desktop, narrow, keyboard, empty, error, permission, dialog, toast states; no no-JS review. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-163 · L1022-1037 · Then: user resolves design decisions; implementer makes the smallest complete change; user supplies light/dark desktop+narrow screenshots; one independent review checks every numbered item against screenshots/diff; at most one remediation of named failures plus user's focused confirmation; user manual acceptance is the page gate; stop before the next page family. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-164 · L1038-1041 · A tiny visual correction uses the short focused workflow with only the verification needed to prove it. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract
- UIS-165 · L1042-1047 · Continuous multi-pass authorization may defer manual acceptance but not implementation, evidence, review or remediation; cleared pages are recorded as awaiting manual approval; the later combined walkthrough covers shared-shell/CSS regressions; no scope expansion, packaging, commits, deployment or blocker bypass. · → docs/UI.md § 10. UI task and review contract

## docs/UI_PAGE_MATRIX.md (756 lines)
Convention: an entry with a letter suffix (e.g. UPM-012a) shares the same source line(s) as its parent entry. Table rows and some paragraphs are single lines holding both an approval state and a rule; the parent entry carries the coverage, the suffixed entries carry the additional rule(s) or state(s) from the same line(s).

## docs/PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK.md (723 lines)
- RB-001 · L1-6 · Runbook targets one Ubuntu 24.04 VPS; the repository selects no VPS, object-storage provider, GitHub plan, DNS registrar or paid tier; production mutation is an operator decision. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-002 · L7-12 · Bootstrap step 1: apply Ubuntu security updates and reboot once; install Docker Engine + Compose v2 plugin from Docker's official Ubuntu repo, plus `restic`, `awscli`, `jq`, `openssh-server`, `openssl`, `ufw`; enable Docker at boot; no deployment framework. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-003 · L13-18 · `/srv/bingo` is a root-owned checkout with reviewed `compose.production.yml`, `deploy/Caddyfile` and release scripts; `/etc/bingo` root-owned mode `0700`; copy `deploy/host/*.example` templates there and make `production.env`, `production-ops.env`, `restic.env`, `restic-password`, `ghcr-readonly.env`, `monitoring.env` `root:root` `0600`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-004 · L19-21 · Checked-in monitoring template has only empty `BINGO_BACKUP_HEARTBEAT_URL` and `BINGO_DISK_HEARTBEAT_URL`; real Better Stack URLs go only in `/etc/bingo/monitoring.env`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-005 · L22-25 · Before a clean first bootstrap create the temporary root-only file named by `BINGO_BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_FILE` with the final owner password; not a config file; removed after successful owner bootstrap, left for retry on failure/interruption. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-006 · L26-29 · Configure and interactively initialize an S3-compatible restic repository; store only its URL, access credentials and password file on host; restic encryption mandatory; repository must be off-host. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-007 · L30-36 · Install `bingo-ops-validation.sh` and `bingo-ops-lib.sh` root-owned `0750` under `/usr/local/libexec/` (side by side); install `bingo-deploy`, `bingo-backup`, `bingo-restore`, `bingo-verify-evidence`, `bingo-check-disk` root-owned `0750` under `/usr/local/sbin/`; install the four `deploy/systemd/` units root-owned `0644` under `/etc/systemd/system/`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-008 · L37-40 · Backup and disk services load `/etc/bingo/monitoring.env`; backup service is the only caller receiving the daily backup heartbeat; reload systemd, enable both timers, verify with `systemctl list-timers bingo-backup.timer bingo-disk-monitor.timer`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-009 · L41-45 · Dedicated SSH deploy user, key only, not in `docker` group; disable password SSH and direct root SSH; UFW allows only TCP 22, 80, 443; exact host public key in GitHub `PRODUCTION_SSH_KNOWN_HOSTS`; strict host-key checking, never accept a changed key. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-010 · L46-50 · Install `deploy/sudoers/bingo-deploy` root-owned `0440` after `visudo` validation, changing only the username; deploy user may run exactly `/usr/local/sbin/bingo-deploy` as root; app/PostgreSQL/R2/Discord/owner/restic/production config secrets never go to GitHub Actions. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-011 · L51-55 · Host GHCR credential is read-only for the package; Caddy and PostgreSQL image tags are controlled host maintenance — routine app deploys never refresh those upstream tags; pin and rehearse host images. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap
- RB-012 · L56-61 · Create `/var/lib/bingo/bootstrap-state` root `0600` containing exactly `new` before first backup/deploy; deploy script sets `interrupted` before first-bootstrap work and `completed` only after production preflight succeeds; never infer state from account count. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 2. One-time host bootstrap / Bootstrap state
- RB-013 · L62-66 · After reboot, Docker, backup timer and disk-monitor timer must be enabled and named volumes present; check `systemctl is-active docker`, `systemctl is-enabled bingo-backup.timer`, `systemctl is-enabled bingo-disk-monitor.timer` before any deployment. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. Repeat deployment and candidate promotion / Before deploying
- RB-014 · L67-70 · Before deployment an authorized operator must pass the AU20 Final Review count gate; docs do not authorize production access. · → dropped: duplicate of RB-053
- RB-015 · L71-81 · `production-promotion.yml` modes `promote`/`deploy`, validation, manual dispatch = approval, no Environment, non-cancelling `production` concurrency, five SSH secrets plus validated metadata. · → dropped: duplicate of TOP-021, TOP-022, TOP-023
- RB-016 · L82-92 · Dispatch checklist: select `production-promotion.yml` on `main` (other refs rejected); supply full `source_sha`, exact `sha256:` `image_digest` and numeric `ci_run_id` from the same successful `main` CI run; choose `promote` (receipt) or `deploy` (approval); validation fails closed before either. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. Repeat deployment and candidate promotion / Promotion workflow
- RB-017 · L93-97 · For one-off Compose operator commands, explicitly set `BINGO_WEB_IMAGE` to the currently deployed immutable image (deploy exports it transiently; `/etc/bingo/production.env` may name an older image). · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 8. Operational procedures / One-off Compose commands
- RB-018 · L98-104 · Remote command is root-owned `bingo-deploy`; fails closed on bad IDs/digests, missing/broad permissions, unavailable Docker/Compose, missing volumes, malformed config, backup/restore, migration, preflight, host-key, public HTTPS or any remote failure; always uses `ghcr.io/<owner>/<repo>@sha256:<digest>`; mutable tags rejected. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. Repeat deployment and candidate promotion / What `bingo-deploy` does
- RB-019 · L105-110 · Deploy step 1: capture running web's immutable image and OCI source revision, stop `web`; stays stopped through backup, migration, owner bootstrap, preflight and new-web readiness; short public interruption accepted. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. Repeat deployment and candidate promotion / What `bingo-deploy` does
- RB-020 · L111-116 · Deploy step 2: validate Compose and all five volumes, start only PostgreSQL if needed, create custom-format dump; manifest binds captured image/source, dump, migration history, DP keys, both configs, bootstrap state and every checksum; restic tags bind backup ID and manifest checksum; local receipt is secret-free corroboration, never a restore prerequisite. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. Repeat deployment and candidate promotion / What `bingo-deploy` does
- RB-021 · L117-125 · Deploy step 3 for `new`/`interrupted`: `--migrate`, owner bootstrap, preflight; new DB is not a production rebuild (preflight needs active activity/item/drop); interrupted owner skipped only after active-owner check; rebuild = `bingo-restore --full-restore` then retained deploy. · → dropped: duplicate of TOP-025, TOP-026, TOP-027
- RB-022 · L126-127 · On `completed` state the script runs the legacy `--slice1-migration-preflight` only when `BINGO_RETAINED_LEGACY_PREFLIGHT=required` (else `skip`), then `--migrate` and `--production-preflight` (with Luck conversion between, see RB-056). · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 4. ## docs/PRODUCTION_TOPOLOGY.md (183 lines)
- TOP-001 · L1-4 · Production is a provider-neutral single-VPS deployment using the reviewed web image and standard Docker Compose. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-002 · L5-11 · Pass 2/3/4 narrative; "provider provisioning and production mutation remain explicit operator decisions"; pointer to runbook. · → dropped: duplicate of docs/DELIVERY_PLAN.md:545-550 (no external account/DNS/production mutation without explicit user approval); rest history
- TOP-003 · L12-17 · `caddy` is the only public entry point: publishes TCP/UDP 80 and 443, obtains/renews HTTPS certs for `BINGO_DOMAIN`, proxies HTTP and WebSocket/SignalR upgrades to `web:8080`, compresses eligible responses with zstd or gzip. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-004 · L18-21 · `web` is one ASP.NET Core replica, reachable only on private `bingo-private` network, takes an immutable image reference in `BINGO_WEB_IMAGE` (normally a GHCR `@sha256:` digest). · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-005 · L22-23 · `postgres` is PostgreSQL 17 on the private network, no published host port; web connects at `postgres:5432`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-006 · L24-29 · Production definition is `compose.production.yml`, Caddy site `deploy/Caddyfile`; standard `reverse_proxy` handles WebSocket upgrades, no separate SignalR proxy path. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-007 · L30-39 · Five explicitly named physical volumes: `bingo-production-postgres` (/var/lib/postgresql/data), `bingo-production-data-protection` (/var/lib/bingo/data-protection-keys), `bingo-production-catalogue-images` (/var/lib/bingo/catalogue-images, OSRS Wiki image cache), `bingo-production-caddy-data` (/data), `bingo-production-caddy-config` (/config). · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Volumes
- TOP-008 · L40-44 · `DataProtection__KeyRingPath` is persisted and validated before the app serves; short-lived `web-init` service sets writable ownership of the mounted directory before `web` starts. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-009 · L45-50 · `deploy/production.env.example` lists names only; real env file stays outside source control or comes from an approved secret mechanism. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-010 · L51-52 · Config group: `BINGO_DOMAIN` (public hostname) and `BINGO_WEB_IMAGE` (immutable image). · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-011 · L53-56 · `BINGO_POSTGRES_DB/USER/PASSWORD` are the single DB authority: Compose builds the quoted app connection from them and host scripts use them for backup, evidence, migration and readiness. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-012 · L57-58 · `BINGO_DISCORD_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`; register callback `/Account/DiscordCallback` on the configured HTTPS domain. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-013 · L59-61 · R2 evidence storage vars: `BINGO_R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `_BUCKET`, `_SERVICE_URL`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-014 · L62-65 · WOM vars: `BINGO_WISE_OLD_MAN_BASE_URL`, `_USER_AGENT`, `_API_KEY` (optional), `_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-015 · L66-71 · `BINGO_BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_FILE` names a temporary root-only file used only by the one-shot initial-owner command, deleted after successful bootstrap, never in the long-running web env; `BINGO_CATALOGUE_IMAGE_SYNC_DELAY_MILLISECONDS` controls catalogue-cache sync. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Configuration
- TOP-016 · L72-77 · Compose sets `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Production`, container port 8080, trusts proxy forwarded HTTPS headers via the ASP.NET container switch, selects R2, points catalogue cache at its volume; no Development fake WOM settings or dev credentials in production. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-017 · L78-82 · Production uses the built-in JSON console logger and does not trust arbitrary external forwarded headers; Caddy is the only public proxy and overwrites forwarded headers. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 1. Topology / Services
- TOP-018 · L83-86 · `/health/live` is public and cheap; `/health/ready` checks PostgreSQL, R2 bucket reachability and timely heartbeats from both hosted workers; Caddy returns 404 for `/health/ready`; Compose probes it on the private container and starts Caddy only after web is healthy. · → docs/OPERATIONS.md § 7. Release gates and deployment contract / Principles
